# Runbook progress — the tracker

**What this is.** The single place that says where the Redkiln-RS runbook stands: which stage is live, which decisions are
recorded, which of the audit's 110 concerns are closed, and what the validators last said. `runbook/INDEX.md` is the map;
this file is the odometer. Update it in the same commit as the work it records; a row that says `done` without a commit
hash beside it is a claim, not a record.

**Legend.** Stage status: `not started` · `in progress` · `checkpoint passed <hash>` · `halted (Halt N)`. PIA disposition:
`open` · `done` (edit landed) · `recorded` (decision landed as ADR/DECISIONS) · `awaiting-ryan` · `deferred (<reason>)` ·
`not-applicable (<reason>)`. Every non-`open` row names where it landed.

---

ADR-0157's own experiment could not have seen it, because run 1 stops at `analysis` with exit 3, so the shipped tree has never taken two boundaries in one process. **(2) A non-zero exit ALONGSIDE an advance was discarded** — the loop read its exit only on the no-advance path, so all four of `story_trailer`'s refusals reached stderr while the process exited on the last stage's status: **ADR-0159's refusal could not fail a run.** **(3) A `[project] name` containing `/` breaks the axis silently** — `split_qualified` takes the FIRST slash, so the mark parses under a different project, the idempotence read never matches, and it re-mints as `duplicate-story-trailer` drift every run. **The missing instrument is now there:** the fixture pack gains a third live stage so a lane can take TWO boundaries, and **mutant M10 — ADR-0157's guard restored exactly — is RED** at `lane_order.rs:555`. A one-hop fixture could not tell the two guards apart, which is why ADR-0157 shipped with the hole. **FIVE ERRATA, all self-inflicted and all one shape** — a SIXTH, `E-0156-1`, was raised at the close and is a different shape entirely (a record contradicting itself rather than drifting from a file)**:** ADR-0152, 0153, 0154, 0155 and 0159 cite file:line into files this stage kept editing. This row's later claims that `ports.toolchain` has no call site and that RT-19 is OWED are STALE: s7g built `run_tool_step`, and RT-19 was settled under D21-5/ADR-0133. ~~***Checkpoint 8 PASSED — but PHASE 1 DOES NOT EXIT.***~~ Four of the six criteria pass and two do not, and both failures are MEASURED rather than assumed. **(2) PASSES** — `rebuild_differential` (AC-184) and `stage_level_next_action` (AC-161) authored UNFEATURED in a new `tests/it/projection.rs`, because `s8`'s filter runs `cargo test -p rk-kernel` with no `--features corpus` and `replay.rs` is corpus-gated whole. **(3) asserted half PASSES** — `kill_and_resume_n_step` in `tests/it/cross_process.rs`, a child process committing 2 of 3 activations to the durable `FileStore` and dying mid-graph at `exit(137)`, with a DIFFERENT process re-entering: ADR-0087 clause 2's real process boundary, which `ac013_re_entry_reexecutes_nothing`'s dropped `Runtime` could not reach. Its operational arm is blocked with (1). **(4) PASSES** after its invocation was repaired. **(6) PASSES on BOTH lints** — the provenance lint prints *derived facets: 6; records read: 6* and two NOT MEASURABLE rows. **(1) NOT SATISFIED, and this is the stage's finding: the pack Step 7e derived does not load, and no `redkiln run` had ever been attempted against it.** The first real run refused at `requires_cycle_lint` — `Requirement` has carried `optional: bool` since it was authored and the lint never read it, so every `Kind?` was a hard edge; measured over all 102 activities the non-optional subgraph has NO cycle, reproducing F35's *hard cycles 0* exactly, while the three mutual pairs are every one both-optional. Fixed on operator decision with a must-LOAD fixture watched failing first. The next run refused again at `RankTie` over `ImplReport` (`hotfix` vs `implement-ue` at rank 10): of the 9 equal-rank producer sets, 5 are on toolchain-keyed kinds and resolved by step-2 expansion, and 2 of the remaining 4 are `ImplReport` exactly shadowing `Changeset`'s producer set because `Changeset` is keyed and `ImplReport` is not — `artifacts.toml` carries precisely the five keyed kinds the design names, so that is pack authoring and 7e's, RAISED not acted on. `redkiln doctor` passes all 39 rules without seeing either defect. Three further structural gaps were measured and none is repaired here: nothing mints a `Story:` or `Slice-Verdict:` trailer (both exist only in the PARSER) and nothing stages the harness's work, so a LANDED run leaves zero commits; `ports.toolchain` has NO call site anywhere in `crates/*/src/`, so AC-473's *toolchain stage through `rk-adapter-toolchain-rust`* has no mechanism; and `run` takes ONE action with `AdvanceStage` unreachable because `ctx.successor` is never set. **(7) NOT RUN**, on two independent blockers: there are zero `dispatch_completed` events because no run completes, AND ADR-0110's batch delta is defined over *readings sharing one `resetsAt`* while NOTHING journals a resetsAt — `EventKind::RateLimitReading` has no producer anywhere in the workspace and `DispatchRecord`'s headroom members are `WindowReading` with no such field. The verb was deliberately NOT built: its only reachable output would have been a vacuous `low_sample` green, which is Halt 8's shape. **RT-19 stays OWED and now has company.** *(2026-09-21. Operator decisions, each taken before the thing it authorised ran: the `--pinned-corpus` amendment that made (6)'s second lint reachable at all — it exited 3 as written, Checkpoint 7e line 141's identical defect; the run-scoped trailer guard, because `git log --all` was satisfied by spike commit `ba46ed9` with ZERO runs performed; the four Checkpoint 8 instrument repairs; the cycle-lint fix; and criterion (4)'s invocation. Every repaired gate carries a synthetic control so its exclusions cannot silently empty it, and two mutant cycles with hash-verified restores proved the new assertions red for the right reason — one of which revealed that `stage_action`'s tier filter is unobservable through `StageAction` by construction, closed by a source lint.)* | Checkpoint 7e | Checkpoint 8 | *(Scoped 2026-09-24, D21-40: what passed here is the **mechanical block**. **Checkpoint 8 has NOT passed**, because s8 defines it as all six criteria plus the standing bullets.)* **mechanical block passed `d6160ff`** (this read *"checkpoint passed"*) — run WHOLE from `s8`'s own bytes: rc=0, **0 `FAIL`**, **46** non-comment statements and **6** `# →`-bearing lines (33 and 6 at the stage's entry; the 13 added are the four repaired instruments' rc-checks and their synthetic controls). The block commits nothing and asserts no cleanliness, unlike 7e's, so no pre-checkpoint commit ordering was forced. Counts re-taken here, not inherited. | **Current blockers (2026-09-24, D21-41 + D21-42).** Every exit-path decision is TAKEN. What stands between the tree and Checkpoint 8 is the sub-stages those decisions make: the widened `Toolchain::test` and the registry verb method; `ChangeSummary`'s `Text` variant and a Phase-1 **S-BIN** run; step-grain commits; the complete resolver; rk-store's render path; the five restored demands and `tag-release`; stage-bearing `Story:` trailers and multi-process advance; kernel-owned git-first tier-1 writes with rebuild in the adapter; Slice-Verdict; the landing checkpoint; the verify record; the ADR-0080 rewrites and severity T; criterion (7)'s AC-395/AC-372 halves and `doctor` reading the store; the catalog checks over `.redkiln/`; **CI green on both OS legs (`ci.yml` still has no pnpm step — ADR-0164's evidence)**; the run environment (pinned model ids, the CLI pin moved once and frozen, the launcher, the kernel spend caps); and the operator's signatures on `gate_expiry` and RT-57. Then the real runs, each on its own go-ahead. **Stale below, and kept as the 2026-09-21 record:** `ports.toolchain` HAS a call site (`run_tool_step`, s7g); the `ImplReport` rank tie was resolved by ADR-0132 (D21-4); `Story:` IS minted and the lane DOES advance within one process (s7h); RT-19 was settled inside ADR-0133 (D21-8); and `resetsAt` IS journalled (ADR-0163). *(2026-09-21 text follows.)* **Phase 1's EXIT, not this stage's checkpoint.** Criterion **(1)** on two catalog defects — one fixed (the optional-edge cycle lint), one RAISED (the `ImplReport` rank tie, pack authoring and 7e's) — and behind them three structural gaps: no `Story:`/`Slice-Verdict:` producer and nothing staging the harness's work, no call site for `ports.toolchain`, and no stage advance. Criterion **(7)** on zero dispatch events AND on ADR-0110's delta being defined over a `resetsAt` that nothing journals. Criterion (3)'s operational arm rides on (1). |

The `activity_id@version` join is recorded as a decision this stage may not take, so a fixture whose catalog ids embedded `@1` would be the test taking it. `replay_resume_seeds_fold_from_journal_and_steps_gate_open` measures the seam and asserts the remaining refusal names `@version`; the coupling is in the module header. **R-38 — `write_run`'s producer on a resumed run. SETTLED 2026-09-20; no ADR, and no code change.** The manifest hard-codes `"producer": "rk-kernel::replay::record"` and stays a literal for a run `resume` produced. **The literal is CORRECT and stays**, on Slice 3's contract critic's GAP 17 verdict, with the reason now carried in `replay.rs` itself: `producer` names the recording **MECHANISM**, not the function that wrote the file. The competing reading is refuted by the literal on EVERY path rather than only the resumed one — `write_run` is what writes `run.json`, and the literal has never said `write_run` — so `resume` exposes a looseness that was already there on a plain `record()` run instead of creating one. `record` and `resume` are two entry points with separate loops (`resume` does not call `record`) that build the same `RecordedRun` in the same layout through the one writer, and that shared layout is what the member names. **`producer` is INFORMATIONAL and gets no instrument**, and so is `event_count`, which sits in the identical position: both are written by `write_run` and read by nothing, and neither may be given a check without first being given a reader. A test pinning the literal would read a hard-coded string back through the writer that hard-codes it, and an `event_count`-against-`events.jsonl` assertion compares a count against the vector it was derived from — both are FLOORS no implementation could violate, reported and not written down as checks (a tamper detector, not a defect detector). Recorded in two places a reader of the manifest reaches: the module header's layout block and a note beside the write. Route judged against **D18-4**'s precedent (*"a stage header-table row under AC-438, **not an ADR**"*): nothing here changes code, mints an instrument, forbids anything, or takes an architecture decision, so a dated disposition is proportionate and ADR-0130 is not the model. **One consequence landed elsewhere the same day:** ADR-0130 decision 8 named this residual *"**R-38**, still open, and untouched here"*, which the settlement falsified, so that clause is **corrected in place before acceptance** on `a94192f`'s precedent (*"still `proposed` … so their bodies are corrected here rather than by supersession — ADR-0092 binds an accepted record only"*). The window was this commit: ADR-0092's `**What this forbids**` **forbids an erratum that touches a `## Decision` sentence** (*"those are supersessions"*) and ERRATA.md's convention scopes errata to Context, Drivers, Options or Evidence, so once ADR-0130 is `accepted` the only lawful repair of two stale words would be a whole superseding record. The clause's normative content — that ADR-0130 does not decide `producer` on a resumed run — is unmoved, which is why this is a correction and not a supersession. ***(Corrected 2026-09-20 at the settlement. The row read **"nothing in the runbook says what a resumed run's producer should be, and the `explain/*/` fixtures and `ac432_explain_over_replayed_log_is_byte_identical` read it."* The final clause was FALSE when written and is refuted by measurement: `grep -rn producer crates/ --include=*.rs` puts the manifest member at exactly two sites — `crates/rk-kernel/src/replay.rs`:98 (the header's layout listing) and its write — every other hit being the unrelated selection-domain sense (*producer of a kind*). `read_run` parses `schema`, `stage`, `run_id`, `inputs_digest`, `terminal_state` and `next_action` and **not** `producer`, so `RecordedRun` has no field for it and `rk-cli`'s `explain`, which renders a `RecordedRun`, cannot print it. The fixtures and `ac432_` **CARRY** the member in their `run.json` bytes and **READ** neither it nor `event_count`. A second premise, offered at the settlement as precedent — that `read_run`'s layout table already documented the two members as not read back — was also FALSE: `grep -rn "not read back" --include=*.rs --include=*.md` hit no source file, so the disposition is AUTHORED here and at the two code sites rather than cited.)* **R-39 — `results_at` is absent from the entire `s7d` stage file** (`grep -n results_at runbook/phase-1/s7d-cli-and-migrate.md` → zero hits) although `derive`'s dispatch envelope calls `runtime.require_results_at()?`, so every dispatching run refuses on `inputs/runtime.json` without it. conflating the two counts is what makes this drift look larger than it is. **Recorded as a dated disposition, with NOTHING to correct in the stage file (ten):** **R-46** (the critic's AC-410 pairing does not reproduce — the refuse-all mutant reddens both halves because the negative asserts refusals BY VARIANT NAME and its two arms demand different variants; the critic is a contract artefact, not a stage-file clause). **R-47** and **R-48** — **RECORDS OWED, and this sweep does not take them**: RT-41/RT-47 (`reconcile_started` carrying `adapter_set`, `config_digest` and the ownership pair, which no design §5 row carries — `s7d` §7d.1 leaves the route open between a dated design §5 note and a `run_started` kind minted by ADR-0116, and the second would contradict the routing already taken onto the existing per-invocation row) and RT-59 (`answer` as the first producer of `artifact.write` anywhere). Both are above this stage and are flagged for the operator. **R-51** (`s7d` §7d.3's *"exit `0`"* for a resumed run needs a COMMITTING transcript; the mechanism landed at cycle 10 as `ExitCase.transcript` and the row is left for whoever revisits `ac264_`). **R-52** (fmt and clippy both RED at `8a02573` — repaired at `f86a92a`; historical, and the standing lesson is that a commit body's *"gates green"* is a claim, measured in a throwaway worktree when blame is ambiguous). **R-53** (`RunInputs::runtime()` is private — `fn runtime(&self)` at `replay.rs`:908, no `pub` — so a CLI needing the recorded tree hash or deadline back must thread them; `s7d` §7d.5 step (9) names neither as a parameter, and the shipped root threads them). **R-57** (`output_digest` has no carrier in the workspace; it is derived over the recorded `activity_committed.result` and DISCLOSED as derived at `explain.rs`:364-371, which is ADR-0128 decision 4's quantity). **R-58** (the prescribed subject's *"AC-402 three paths"* overstates — Slice 3 discharges the negative plus the `run` path and `ac402_reconcile_called_on_run_answer_doctor` is Slice 4's; the subject was used VERBATIM anyway, per `s7d` §7d.16 item 3's own instruction, and Slice 4 is what makes the claim true). **R-61** (GAP 21's account of the second `11` is the contract's, not this file's; both `11` rows are the adapter's, and neither GAP 21's row set nor `s7d` §7d.4's enumeration carries a `14` — left as measured). **Also corrected in the same pass, from the Slice 4 contract:** `s7d` §7d.16 item 4's fixture count read **thirty-two** and is **thirty-eight** — D18-19 moved the table 32 → 38 on 2026-09-19 and item 4 was not updated with it, while `s7d` §7d.8 and `s7d` §7d.13 both say thirty-eight and an independent count off the table's first column agrees (39 tokens less the `RK-D-DANGLING-` prose prefix). AC-415 is a set equality in BOTH directions, so building thirty-two leaves it red. ***R-47 and R-48 SETTLED 2026-09-20 by dated design §5 notes, on the operator's decision of that date, each route chosen against a measurement rather than a preference.*** **R-47** — design §5's `reconcile_started` row now carries `adapter_set`, `config_digest` (RT-41) and `RunOwnership { worktree, branch }` (RT-47) beside invocation id, HEAD and the drift set. **The alternative was FORECLOSED, not merely heavier**: minting the `run_started` kind AC-485's THEN names literally edits `crates/rk-types/src/event_kind.rs`, and `s7d` §7d.15's floor is that *"`rk-types`, `rk-ports`, `rk-store`, `rk-git` and the four adapters are not edited"* — so the *"either/or"* `s7d` §7d.1 left open had one live arm. **The note states the tier-2 argument rather than assuming it**: ADR-0109 decision 2 requires that dropping the event store not change `run`'s next action, so every member of a tier-2 row must be recomputable without it, and all three are (`adapter_set` and `config_digest` from `.redkiln/config.toml`; the worktree and branch deterministically from the item id). **R-48** — design §5's `artifact.write` producing step now reads *"every activity output, and `redkiln answer` for an admitted attachment"*. **A supersession was considered and is NOT owed, on ADR-0116's own forbids block**: it reads *"Forbids an activity output with no `artifact.write` event"* — a **FLOOR** on where the row is required, not a **CEILING** on who may write one — so the producing-step column states an obligation, a further producer is an addition the record neither mandates nor forbids, no `## Decision` sentence is withdrawn, and ADR-0092's bar on an erratum touching a Decision does not bite. ***What these two notes UNBLOCK, and what they deliberately do NOT claim.*** `s7d` §7d.18 holds **AC-485 PROVISIONAL** (*"the carrier is routed, RT-41"*) and **AC-529's CLI end ARMED** (*"claimed only once the ownership carrier record (RT-47) lands"*). That condition is now met and both are **UNBLOCKED** — but **neither is discharged here**: a criterion is claimed by the slice or stage close that runs its instrument, not by the commit that lands the record it waited on (ADR-0101 clause 5). **R-63 — RAISED by this work, not absorbed.** AC-529's THEN is absolute — *"a tier-1 write resolves the run-owned worktree **from the store**, regardless of cwd"* — and the two verbs do it differently [measured 2026-09-20]. **`answer` resolves FROM THE STORE and is the criterion's path**: `recorded_ownership` reads the pair back off the last `reconcile_started` row and the refusal names *"the store, never from the working directory"* [`verbs/answer.rs`:255-270, :583-596]. **`run`'s own `Gate:` trailer write DERIVES it** — `<common-dir>/redkiln/worktrees/<item>` on `rk-run/<item>`, built in the same invocation that re-creates the worktree at step (8) and writes the row at step (6) [`verbs/run.rs`:1235-1245]. That is defensible (there is nothing to read back at creation time, and the value is identical to the row's) and it is **not** a cwd resolution, so AC-518 is untouched; but whether AC-529's *"from the store"* binds a same-invocation tier-1 write is **not settled by these notes** and is left for the close, which runs the instrument. Raised rather than re-counted, on `s7d` §7d.16 item 3's own rule. | **RAISED 2026-09-20. SWEPT 2026-09-20** — R-44, R-45, R-49, R-50, R-55, R-56, R-59 and R-60 **CORRECTED IN `s7d`** by dated amendment; R-46, R-51, R-52, R-53, R-57, R-58 and R-61 **DISPOSED by dated note**, nothing in the stage file to correct; **R-47 and R-48 SETTLED 2026-09-20** by dated design §5 notes on the operator's decision (the `reconcile_started` payload's three added members; `artifact.write`'s widened producer) — **unblocking AC-485 and AC-529's CLI end without claiming either**, and **raising R-63** — except **R-40…R-43**, which are **REPAIRED** in the commits named; **R-54**, which is **REPAIRED 2026-09-20** by **ADR-0130** (*`accepted` 2026-09-20 on the operator's dated line `DECISIONS-2026-09-20` **D20-1**; it was `proposed` when this row was written*) and its implementation, `write_run` writing a conditional `inputs/config.json` and `read_run` restoring it through `Config::new` — guarded by `-p rk-kernel`'s `recorded_log::write_run_and_read_run_round_trip_every_recorded_input`, which compares two `run_id`s over the WHOLE preimage rather than enumerating members, so the next input added to `to_json` and not to the layout reddens it without anyone extending a list; and **R-62**, which is **CLOSED 2026-09-20** by a dated amendment to `s7b` §7b.14 widening both exclusions to `^crates/(rk-kernel\|rk-cli)/tests/fixtures/` on the operator's decision, after which the block runs rc=0 whole | `s7d` §7d.3, `s7d` §7d.4, `s7d` §7d.6, `s7d` §7d.7, `s7d` §7d.16 item 3; `s7b` §7b.14 (amended); `references/adr/0130-the-recorded-config-is-written-to-the-run-record-layout.md`; `crates/rk-cli/tests/fixtures/exit/README.md`; the cycle 7–10 commit bodies | awaiting-ryan |

🛑 **No erratum was written this pass, and three owed ones are withdrawn AS INSTRUMENTS.** Under
**`DECISIONS-2026-09-15 D15-2`** a criterion correction takes a **dated in-place register amendment**, because a
criterion in an amendable register is not a frozen record — `ERRATA.md`:273-276 concedes the route in its own words
and `s7b` §7b.11 precondition 2 asks for exactly that and names no erratum at all. **`E-0071-1`**, **`E-0079-2`** and
**`E-0105-1`** are withdrawn **as instruments and not as obligations**: each correction still lands, in place and
dated. The correction owed against **AC-484**'s third instrument takes the same form. **Nothing was written to
`references/adr/ERRATA.md`, its convention header is unchanged, and `E-0520-1` and every existing erratum are
untouched.** Every site that said an erratum was owed is re-pointed — including §1's row 7b above and golden
constraint 1 in `00-preamble.md`, whose stale *"ADR-0079 is `proposed`"* label is now a dated in-place correction.

**Six residuals this pass did not settle**, named at `spikes/DECISIONS-2026-09-15.md` so nobody rediscovers them:

- **R-1** — **AC-389's second THEN half (*"the attempt is appended as an event"*) has no event kind to be appended
  as.** `EventKind` is closed at 29, ADR-0112's eleven `StepError` variants carry no member for a refused appetite
  raise, and design §5's table has no row; the crate says so in `crates/rk-kernel/tests/it/budget.rs`’s own module doc, under its heading *"AND AC-389's OTHER HALF IS STILL UNBUILT AND STILL OWED A RECORD"* — **cited by that heading and checkable with `grep -cF`, never by line**: the line range carried here from `spikes/DECISIONS-2026-09-15.md` R-1 lands in AC-022’s fan-out assertions instead, and the single line number that replaced it was wrong twice over — it named one site for a phrase that occurs at two. The **29** and the **eleven** are `rk-types`’ and ADR-0112’s to own and are not re-derived here.
- **R-2** — **ADR-0122 decision 5(c)**, the one lettered item of five that did not land.
- **R-3** — **§4's scope note**, *"Everything in the criteria set is configuration-time and spends no dispatch"*, is imprecise once AC-532
  lands in that area. Named by **D15-3** and deliberately not edited by it.
- **R-4** — ~~**AC-294's third instrument is NOT built.**~~ **PARTLY discharged 2026-09-15, in the same
  session that raised it, and STILL OPEN on its other half** — `spikes/DECISIONS-2026-09-15.md`’s own R-4 row
  says exactly that, having corrected a first wording it describes as *"flatly, which overstated it against the
  register's own more careful wording"*. The module-boundary lint was built test-first as
  `crates/rk-kernel/src/module_boundary.rs` with `crates/rk-kernel/tests/it/module_boundary.rs`
  (`ac294_run_state_construction_sites_are_module_bounded`), and it **scans code lines only and proves it**: a

  authority on that half; no count of it is restated here.** Checkpoint 8's new line asserts the **case file**, not the lint.
- **R-5** — **a record routing `ActivitySpec` assembly.** D-11 settled the *build scope* (7c); the record is owed.

This is the same family as the 7a defect where two agents shared a working tree, reached from the sequential direction rather than the concurrent one: **a phase boundary is not a guarantee that the previous phase left the tree usable.** The fix phase repaired the module and every gate is green, but the three verdicts from that run are **low-confidence by construction** and were treated as such. **The repair is a green-gate between build and review** — a phase that fails the build must not hand a tree to a reviewer — and it is owed to the next workflow, not to a file. *(And the caller's first diagnosis was wrong: "a test written into `src/`". `replay.rs` is a legitimate runtime-boundary module — it opens files, and `crate::select` denies that — so the fix was to repair the two bugs, not to move the file.)* |

The parent re-execs the test binary as a child writer; both write 40 transactions × 40 rows × 4 KiB as **different items**; both complete with **zero lock errors**, all 3,200 rows present, **one** store file. **And it discriminates**: with `busy_timeout = 0` the test fails **3 of 3 runs** with the parent hitting `database is locked`, and at 5000 ms it passes. So `busy_timeout` is **measured** to be load-bearing at this contention level rather than asserted. **Limit, stated:** one machine, one filesystem (NTFS), one run. It does **not** license claiming 5000 ms suffices under the self-hosting run's load — ADR-0105's own evidence limits reserve that for Step 8 (3). |

| **Where an erratum may reach, settled on precedent rather than on reading (2026-09-13)** | Both sentences sit in `## Decision`, and ADR-0092 says a correction that *moves* a Decision sentence *"is a supersession and takes a new record"* | The question is whether an erratum may touch a line **inside** `## Decision` at all. Measured against the existing set rather than argued: of the 34 lines the errata cite, **two are Decision lines** — ADR-0067:48 and ADR-0086:55 — and **E-0086-1** is squarely on point, withdrawing *"Line 55's instruction to propagate the versions"* while closing *"Decision unchanged — the four crates are excluded on ADR-0003's grounds, which do not depend on version currency."* So the convention in practice is ADR-0092's literal test: does **every sentence of `## Decision` still stand**? Not: does the correction fall inside that section? For E-0100-1 the normative sentence, its consequence and its forbids bullet all stand, so an erratum is right and a supersession would be an over-reaction. The precedent is cited inside the entry so the next reader does not have to re-derive it. |
